\deg(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Y})>0
because
Y
is of general type.
∎

Corollary 5.7
.

Let
X,Y\subset A
be reduced subvarieties, possibly reducible. If the Gauss maps
\gamma_{X}
,
\gamma_{Y}
are both finite morphisms, then

\dim\pi(\Supp(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{X}\circ\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Y}))\;=\;\min\{\dim X+\dim Y,\dim A-1\},

where
\pi\colon A\times\mathbb{P}_{A}\to\mathbb{P}_{A}
is the projection.

Proof.

Combine
lemma

5.5
and
lemma

5.6
.
∎

5.3.
Clean characteristic cycles

From now on and until the end of this section, we work over
k=\mathbb{C}
. Recall that to any perverse sheaf
P\in\textup{Perv}(A,\mathbb{C})
one may attach a characteristic cycle
[
Dim04
, definition 4.3.19]
, a finite formal sum of conormal varieties

\CC(P)\;=\;\sum_{Z\subset A}m_{Z}(P)\cdot\Lambda_{Z}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{with}\hskip 10.00002ptm_{Z}(P)\;\in\;\mathbb{N}.

Here the sum runs over all integral subvarieties
Z\subset A
, and only finitely many
m_{Z}(P)
are nonzero. These cycles contain a lot of information, e.g., the Dubson-Kashiwara index formula shows that we can read off the topological Euler characteristic as

\chi(A,P)\;=\;\sum_{Z\subset A}m_{Z}(P)\cdot\deg(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z})

where
\deg(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z})
is the degree of the Gauss map from
section

2.2
, see
[
FK00
]
. Passing from
CC(P)
to its projectivization and discarding all components which are not clean, we define the
clean characteristic cycle
by

\cc(P)\;:=\;\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textup{$Z\subset A$ of}\\
\textup{general type}\end{subarray}}m_{Z}(P)\cdot\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}.

It contains all information needed for the Dubson-Kashiwara index formula. This index formula implies that for
P\in\textup{Perv}(A,\mathbb{C})
we have
\cc(P)=0
if and only if
P\in\textup{S}(A,\mathbb{C})
. So the clean characteristic cycle of perverse sheaves is defined on the abelian quotient category
\overline{\textup{Perv}}(A,\mathbb{C})=\textup{Perv}(A,\mathbb{C})/\textup{S}(A,\mathbb{C})
. By additivity in short exact sequences we then obtain a group homomorphism from the Grothendieck group of this abelian quotient category to the group of clean cycles:

\cc\colon\hskip 10.00002ptK(\overline{\textup{Perv}}(A,\mathbb{C}))\;\longrightarrow\;\mathcal{L}(A)

The Grothendieck group of an abelian tensor category is not just an abelian group, but also a ring with the product given by the tensor product, which in our case is the convolution product
*
of perverse sheaves. By
[
Krä21
, th. 2.1.1 and ex. 1.3.2]
we have

\cc(P_{1}*P_{2})\;=\;\cc(P_{1})\circ\cc(P_{2})\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{for all}\hskip 10.00002ptP_{1},P_{2}\;\in\;\overline{\textup{Perv}}(A,\mathbb{C}),

where
\circ
is the convolution product of clean cycles introduced previously.

Example 5.8
.

Passing to characteristic cycles is useful since the convolution of clean cycles is easier to control than the convolution of perverse sheaves. For instance, in
corollary

3.5
we have seen that for any perverse sheaf
P\in\textup{Perv}(A,\mathbb{C})
the connected component of its Tannaka group can be realized as the Tannaka group of
[d]_{*}P
for any integer
d\geqslant 1
with
d\cdot\Gamma_{P}=\{0\}
. Here

\Gamma_{P}\;:=\;\bigl\{a\in A(\mathbb{C})_{\textup{tors}}\mid\delta_{a}\;\in\;\langle P\rangle\bigr\}\;\subset\;A(\mathbb{C})

is a finite abelian group, but usually hard to control. However, we have
\Gamma_{P}\subset\Gamma_{\cc(P)}
for

\Gamma_{\cc(P)}\;:=\;\bigl\{a\in A(\mathbb{C})_{\textup{tors}}\mid\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{\{a\}}\;\in\;\langle\cc(P)\rangle\bigr\},

and this latter group depends only on the characteristic cycle of the given perverse sheaf. This will be useful in the proof of part (2) in
theorem

5.11
below.

5.4.
Highest weight theory

We want to use characteristic cycles to study the tensor category generated by a given semisimple perverse sheaf
P\in\textup{Perv}(A,\mathbb{C})
. To pass from Tannaka groups to their connected component, we introduce the following notation:

Definition 5.9
.

Let
m\geqslant 1
be the smallest positive integer with
m\cdot\Gamma_{\cc(P)}=\{0\}
, and

P^{\circ}\;:=\;[m]_{*}P.

Fix a fiber functor
\xi\colon\langle P^{\circ}\rangle\to\Vect(\mathbb{C})
, and consider the fiber functor obtained as the composite

\omega\colon\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox to176.03pt{\vbox to20.17pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 88.0127pt\lower-8.65973pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{

Its associated Tannaka groups are

G\;:=\;G_{\omega}(P)\;\supseteq\;G^{\circ}\;=\;G_{\xi}(P^{\circ}),

where the rightmost equality follows from
corollary

3.5
.

Recall that
G
is a reductive group over
\mathbb{C}
and
\omega
induces an equivalence of abelian tensor categories

\omega:\hskip 10.00002pt\langle P\rangle\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\;\textup{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(G).

In what follows, we will assume that the perverse sheaf
P\in\textup{Perv}(A,\mathbb{C})
is nondivisible and
\det(P)=\delta_{0}
(the latter can be achieved by replacing
P
with a translate). Then by
proposition

3.3
and
corollary

3.5
the connected component
G^{\circ}
is a semisimple group. By highest weight theory, its representation ring has the form

\textup{R}(G^{\circ})\;:=\;K(\textup{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(G^{\circ}))\;=\;\mathbb{Z}[\mathsf{X}]^{W}

where
\mathsf{X}:=\Hom(T,\mathbb{G}_{m})
is the character group of a maximal torus
T\subset G
, endowed with the natural action of the Weyl group
W=N_{G}(T)/Z_{G}(T)
, and we denote by
\mathbb{Z}[\mathsf{X}]^{W}\subset\mathbb{Z}[\mathsf{X}]
the subring of Weyl group invariants. Recall that for semisimple groups the universal cover inherits the structure of an algebraic group and the covering map is an isogeny

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p\colon\hskip-11.0pt}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\circ}\;\subset\;G.}

Hence,
\tilde{T}:=p^{-1}(T)
is a maximal torus in
\tilde{G}
, and
p
induces an isomorphism of Weyl groups

N_{\tilde{G}}(\tilde{T})/\tilde{T}\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\;N_{G}(T)/T\;=:\;W

by means of which these groups are identified in what follows. Moreover,
p
embeds the character group as a subgroup
\mathsf{X}\subset\tilde{\mathsf{X}}:=\Hom(\tilde{T},\mathbb{G}_{m})
of finite index.

Definition 5.10
.

We denote by
d\geqslant 1
the smallest positive integer with
d\cdot\tilde{\mathsf{X}}\subset\mathsf{X}
.

The multiplication by
d
then gives a morphism
[d]\colon\tilde{\mathsf{X}}\to\mathsf{X}
, and we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{R}(G^{\circ})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{R}(\tilde{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{R}(G^{\circ})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[\mathsf{X}]^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[\tilde{\mathsf{X}}]^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[\mathsf{X}]^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[d]_{*}}

where the top row is the
d
-th Adams operation
\Psi_{d}:\textup{R}(G^{\circ})\to\textup{R}(G^{\circ})
.
Even though the universal cover
\tilde{G}
might not be realized as the Tannaka group of a perverse sheaf, we can relate its representations to clean cycles as follows (here we say that a statement holds for a
very general
point of
\mathbb{P}_{A}(\mathbb{C})
if it holds for all points outside a countable union of proper subvarieties):

Theorem 5.11
.

Let
m,d\geqslant 1
be as above.

(1)

The following diagram of ring homomorphisms is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{R}(G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{R}(G^{\circ})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{R}(\tilde{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{R}(G^{\circ})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\langle P\rangle)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\langle P^{\circ}\rangle)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\langle P^{\circ}\rangle)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{\rvert G^{\circ}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cc}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[m]_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cc}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cc}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[m]_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[d]_{*}}

(2)

For very general
v\in\mathbb{P}_{A}(\mathbb{C})
, there is a group homomorphism
\varphi_{v}\colon\mathsf{X}\to A(\mathbb{C})
such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R(G^{\circ})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[\mathsf{X}]^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[\mathsf{X}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[A(\mathbb{C})]=Z_{0}(A)\hskip-50.00008pt}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cc\circ\xi^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1608146}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1608146}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{979321}{219435}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{699516}{156740}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\mapsto\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1608146}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1608146}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{979321}{219435}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{699516}{156740}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{v}}

where
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{v}
is the fiber of the Gauss map
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\to\mathbb{P}_{A}
seen as a
0
-cycle on
A
.

Proof.

See
[
Krä21
, th. 2.2.3]
. For part (2), let
\Gamma\leqslant A(\mathbb{C})
be the subgroup generated by the points in the fiber of the Gauss map
\gamma\colon\cc(P^{\circ})\to\mathbb{P}_{A}
over a very general point
v
, then loc. cit. gives
\varphi_{v}\colon\mathsf{X}\to\Gamma/\Gamma_{\textup{tors}}
with the required properties. It then only remains to note that in our situation the group
\Gamma
is free since our assumption
m\cdot\Gamma_{\cc(P)}=\{0\}
implies that the subring
\langle\cc(P^{\circ})\rangle\subset\mathcal{L}(A)
does not contain any conormal variety to a torsion point in
A(\mathbb{C})
.
∎

Definition 5.12
.

For
\beta\in\tilde{\mathsf{X}}
let
[\beta]\in\mathbb{Z}[\tilde{\mathsf{X}}]
denote the corresponding basis vector in the group algebra. Note that the multiplication of basis vectors in the group algebra is defined by
[\alpha]\cdot[\beta]=[\alpha+\beta]
, and
[\alpha+\beta]\neq[\alpha]+[\beta]
.
The subring
\mathbb{Z}[\tilde{\mathsf{X}}]^{W}\subset\mathbb{Z}[\tilde{\mathsf{X}}]
of Weyl group invariants has as its underlying additive group the free abelian group with
\mathbb{Z}
-basis consisting of the vectors

[W.\alpha]\;:=\;\sum_{\beta\in W.\alpha}[\beta]\;\in\;\mathbb{Z}[\tilde{\mathsf{X}}]^{W}

where
\alpha
runs through the dominant integral weights in
\tilde{\mathsf{X}}
and
W.\alpha\subset\tilde{\mathsf{X}}
denotes its orbit under the Weyl group. Multiplying by the integer
d
from above, we obtain an element

[W.d\alpha]\in\mathbb{Z}[\mathsf{X}]^{W}=\textup{R}(G^{\circ})
. Applying the inverse of
\xi\colon K(\langle P^{\circ}\rangle)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\textup{R}(G^{\circ})
to this element of the representation ring and taking its characteristic cycle, we obtain a clean cycle

\cc(P,\alpha)\;:=\;\cc(\xi^{-1}[W.d\alpha])\;\in\;\mathcal{L}(A).

Note that for any integer
n\neq 0
we have
\cc(P,n\alpha)=[n]_{*}\cc(P,\alpha)
.

Remark 5.13
.

By construction,
\cc(P,\alpha)
lies in the subring
\langle\cc(P^{\circ})\rangle\subset\mathcal{L}(A)
. In particular, if the cycle
\cc(P)
is defined over a given algebraically closed subfield of
\mathbb{C}
, then so is
\cc(P,\alpha)
.

Lemma 5.14
.

For any
\alpha\in\tilde{\mathsf{X}}
the cycle
\cc(P,\alpha)\in\mathcal{L}(A)
is effective. Moreover, with
m
and
d
as in
definition

5.9
and
definition

5.10
, we have

[dm]_{*}\cc(Q)\;=\;\sum_{\alpha}m_{\alpha}(V)\cdot\cc(P,\alpha)\hskip 10.00002pt

for any
Q\in\langle P\rangle
and
V=\omega(Q)\in\textup{Rep}(G)
with weight multiplicities
m_{\alpha}(V)\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Recall that the homomorphism
\varphi_{v}\colon\mathbb{Z}[\mathsf{X}]\to\mathbb{Z}[A(\mathbb{C})]
is induced by the group homomorphism
\varphi_{v}\colon\mathsf{X}\to A(\mathbb{C})
in part (2) of
theorem

5.11
. Hence, it sends the submonoid
\mathbb{N}[\mathsf{X}]\subset\mathbb{Z}[\mathsf{X}]
into the submonoid
\mathbb{N}[A(\mathbb{C})]
of effective
0
-cycles. This construction works for
very
general
v\in\mathbb{P}_{A}(\mathbb{C})
only. However, if a clean cycle is known to have effective fibers over a very general cotangent vector
v\in\mathbb{P}_{A}(\mathbb{C})
, then the cycle is effective.
Hence, the claim about effectivity follows. The formula for multiplicities holds by construction.
∎

By
lemma

5.14
, the weight multiplicities
m_{\alpha}(V)
give us information about the multiplicities in characteristic cycles and vice versa. For example, we say that a representation of a connected reductive group is
minuscule
if it is an irreducible nontrivial representation whose weights for a maximal torus form a single orbit under the Weyl group. A representation of an arbitrary reductive group is called minuscule if its restriction to the connected component of the identity is so. This is a very restrictive condition: For the simple Dynkin types the table in
section

1.4
shows that the only minuscule representations other than standard representations of classical groups are the wedge powers of the standard representation in type
A
, spin and half-spin representations in types
B
and
D
, and the representations of dimension
27
and
56
of the exceptional groups of type
E_{6}
and
E_{7}
. The previous lemma shows that for any perverse sheaf whose characteristic cycle is integral, the corresponding representation must be minuscule
[
Krä22
, cor. 1.10]
:

Corollary 5.15
.

Let
Y\subset A
be a nondivisible subvariety, and let
P\in\textup{Perv}(A,\mathbb{C})
be a simple perverse sheaf with clean characteristic cycle
\cc(P)=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Y}
. Then

\omega(P)
is a minuscule representation of the group
G=G_{\omega}(P)
.

Proof.

Since
Y
is nondivisible, the cycle
[dm]_{*}\cc(P)
is integral. In the above identity for the weight multiplicities of the representation
V=\omega(\delta_{Y})
then
m_{\alpha}(V)\neq 0
for at most one dominant weight
\alpha
, and this weight must enter with multiplicity one.
∎

We now want to give a geometric description of the cycles
\cc(P,\alpha)\in\mathcal{L}(A)
in cases when
G=G_{\omega}(P)
is a classical group. The idea is to express arbitrary weights in terms of the weights in the standard representation, similar to the argument in
[
Krä20
]
. We do this for each of the classical Dynkin types
A,B,D
separately; the computation for the Dynkin type
C
is similar, but we omit it since in type
C
there are no minuscule representations other than the standard representation.

5.5.
Weyl orbits for type
A

For
\tilde{G}=\SL_{n}
, let us denote by
\varepsilon_{1},\dots,\varepsilon_{n}\in\tilde{\mathsf{X}}
the weights of the standard representation. The triviality of the determinant implies that
\varepsilon_{1}+\cdots+\varepsilon_{n}=0
, and the wedge powers of the standard representation have as highest weights the fundamental weights

\varpi_{i}\;=\;\varepsilon_{1}+\cdots+\varepsilon_{i}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{for $0<i<n$}.

The dominant integral weights are the
\mathbb{N}
-linear combinations of the fundamental weights, i.e., the weights

\alpha\;=\;\sum_{\nu=1}^{n-1}\alpha_{\nu}\varepsilon_{\nu}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{with integers}\hskip 10.00002pt\alpha_{1}\geqslant\cdots\geqslant\alpha_{n-1}\geqslant 0.

More generally, for
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, let
\ell=\max\{\nu\mid\alpha_{\nu}\neq 0\}
be the length of the
n
-tuple
\alpha
, and let
I(\ell,n)
be the set of injective maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota\colon\hskip-11.0pt}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{1,\dots,\ell\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{1,\dots,n\}.}

Then
\alpha
has the Weyl group orbit

W.\alpha\;=\;\bigl\{\alpha_{1}\varepsilon_{\iota(1)}+\cdots+\alpha_{\ell}\varepsilon_{\iota(\ell)}\in\tilde{\mathsf{X}}\mid\iota\in I(\ell,n)\bigr\}.

Each weight in this orbit is obtained for precisely
N(\alpha)
distinct choices of
\iota\in I(\ell,n)
, where

N(\alpha)\;:=\;\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\ell_{i}!\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{with}\hskip 10.00002pt\ell_{i}\;:=\;\#\{\nu\mid\alpha_{\nu}=i\},

with the convention
0!=1
so that the above product is finite. Hence, we have

[W.\alpha]\;=\;\frac{1}{N(\alpha)}\cdot\sum_{\iota\in I(\ell,n)}\bigl[\alpha_{1}\varepsilon_{\iota(1)}+\cdots+\alpha_{\ell}\varepsilon_{\iota(\ell)}\bigr]\;\in\;\mathbb{Z}[\mathsf{X}].

To describe the clean cycle
\cc(P,\alpha)
in these terms, we need some more notation:

Definition 5.16
.

Let
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\in\mathcal{L}(A)
be a reduced clean cycle. Let
U\subset\mathbb{P}_{A}
be any open dense subset over which the Gauss map
\gamma_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{972299}{219436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{694499}{156740}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}}\colon\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\to\mathbb{P}_{A}
restricts to a finite flat morphism, and let
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{\mid U}:=\gamma_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{972299}{219436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{694499}{156740}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}}^{-1}(U)
be its preimage. For an integer
\ell\geqslant 1
consider the fiber product

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\times\ell}_{|U}\;:=\;\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{|U}\times_{U}\times\cdots\times_{U}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{|U}\;\subset\;A^{\ell}\times U

and inside it the big diagonal

\Delta_{\ell}\;:=\;\{(p_{1},\dots,p_{\ell},v)\mid p_{i}=p_{j}\;\textnormal{for some $(i,j)$ with $i\neq j$}\}\;\subset\;\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\times\ell}_{|U}.

Since the fiber product of finite flat morphisms is again a finite flat morphism, every irreducible component of the fiber product
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\times\ell}_{\mid U}
is a finite flat cover of
U
. So the Zariski closure

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell]}\;:=\;\overline{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\times\ell}_{\mid U}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\ell}}\;\subset\;A^{\ell}\times\mathbb{P}_{A}

does not depend on the specific choice of the open dense subset
U\subset\mathbb{P}_{A}
over which
\gamma_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{972299}{219436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{694499}{156740}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}}
is finite and flat. For any
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{\ell})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\ell}
we then define a clean cycle as the pushforward

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\alpha}\;:=\;\frac{1}{N(\alpha)}\cdot\sigma_{\alpha\ast}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell]})\;\in\;\mathcal{L}(A)

for the ‘sum’ morphism

\sigma_{\alpha}\colon A^{\ell}\times\mathbb{P}_{A}\longrightarrow A\times\mathbb{P}_{A},\hskip 10.00002pt(p_{1},\dots,p_{\ell},v)\mapsto(\alpha_{1}p_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{\ell}p_{\ell},v).

Remark 5.17
.

The group
\mathfrak{S}_{\ell}
acts on
A^{\ell}\times\mathbb{P}_{A}
by permutations of the abelian variety factors, and this action restricts to an action on
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell]}\subset A^{\ell}\times\mathbb{P}_{A}
. The morphism
\sigma_{\alpha}
factors through the quotient by the subgroup

\mathfrak{S}_{\alpha}\;:=\;\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{S}_{\ell_{i}}\;\subset\;\mathfrak{S}_{\ell}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{where}\hskip 10.00002pt\ell_{i}\;:=\;\#\{\nu\mid\alpha_{\nu}=i\},

as shown in the following commutative diagram:

A^{\ell}\times\mathbb{P}_{A}
A\times\mathbb{P}_{A}
(A^{\ell}\times\mathbb{P}_{A})/\mathfrak{S}_{\alpha}
\scriptstyle\sigma_{\alpha}
\scriptstyle q

\scriptstyle\exists!\tilde{\sigma}_{\alpha}

Here, the quotient morphism
q
is finite of degree
N(\alpha)
. The restriction of
q
to the subvariety
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell]}\subset A^{\ell}\times\mathbb{P}_{A}
is still finite of the same degree over its image, as one may see by looking at a general fiber of the Gauss map. Hence,
definition

5.16
amounts to

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\alpha}\;=\;\tilde{\sigma}_{\alpha\ast}(\tilde{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}}^{\alpha})\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{where}\hskip 10.00002pt\tilde{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}}^{\alpha}\;:=\;\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell]}/\mathfrak{S}_{\alpha}\;\subset\;(A^{\ell}\times\mathbb{P}_{A})/\mathfrak{S}_{\alpha}.

In particular, it follows that if
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\alpha}
is reduced resp. integral, then so is
\tilde{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}}^{\alpha}
.

We now obtain from
theorem

5.11
the following description of the clean cycles corresponding to the dominant weights:

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
G=G_{\omega}(P)
be semisimple with universal cover
\tilde{G}\simeq\SL_{n}
, and let
\alpha\in\tilde{\mathsf{X}}
be a dominant weight. If the clean cycle
\cc(P,\alpha)
is reduced resp. integral, then

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}:=\cc(P,\varepsilon_{1})

is reduced resp. integral, and

\cc(P,\alpha)\;=\;\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\alpha}

where on the right-hand side we identify
\alpha=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{i}\varepsilon_{i}
with the tuple
(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n})
.

Proof.

Let
v\in\mathbb{P}_{A}
be a very general cotangent direction, and let
p_{1},\dots,p_{n}\in A(\mathbb{C})
be the points in the fiber of the Gauss map
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\to\mathbb{P}_{A}
over this direction, counted with multiplicities so that

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{v}\;=\;[p_{1}]+\cdots+[p_{n}]

inside
\mathbb{Z}[A(\mathbb{C})]
. Recall from
theorem

5.11
that the points in the fiber are precisely the images of the weights in the Weyl group orbit
W\cdot d\varepsilon_{1}=\{d\varepsilon_{1},\dots,d\varepsilon_{n}\}\subset\mathsf{X}
under the homomorphism
\varphi_{v}\colon\mathsf{X}\to A(\mathbb{C})
. Up to relabelling indices, we may assume that
\varphi_{v}(d\varepsilon_{i})=p_{i}
for all
i
. Writing the weight as
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{\ell})
with
\ell<n
, we have

\displaystyle\cc(P,\alpha)_{v}\;=\;\cc(\xi^{-1}[W.d\alpha])_{v}

\displaystyle\;=\;

\displaystyle\varphi_{v}([W.d\alpha])

\displaystyle\;=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N(\alpha)}\cdot\sum_{\iota\in I(\ell,n)}[\varphi_{v}(\alpha_{1}d\varepsilon_{\iota(1)}+\cdots+\alpha_{\ell}d\varepsilon_{\iota(\ell)})]

\displaystyle\;=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N(\alpha)}\cdot\sum_{\iota\in I(\ell,n)}[\alpha_{1}p_{\iota(1)}+\cdots+\alpha_{\ell}p_{\iota(\ell)}].

For very general
v
this
0
-cycle contains no multiple points, since we assumed
\cc(P,\alpha)
to be reduced. Therefore, each point

p\;=\;\alpha_{1}p_{\iota(1)}+\cdots+\alpha_{\ell}p_{\iota(\ell)}

enters in the above expression for
\cc(P,\alpha)_{v}
only for
N(\alpha)
different choices of
\iota
. But on the other hand
p
does not change if we replace the map
\iota\colon\{1,\dots,\ell\}\hookrightarrow\{1,\dots,n\}
by
\iota\circ\tau
for any permutation

\tau\;\in\;\mathfrak{S}_{\alpha}\;=\;\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{S}_{\ell_{i}}\;\subset\;\mathfrak{S}_{\ell}.

Since
N(\alpha)=|\mathfrak{S}_{\alpha}|
, it follows from the above that for all
\iota,\iota^{*}\in I(\ell,n)
we have the equivalence

\sum_{\nu=1}^{\ell}\alpha_{\nu}p_{\iota(\nu)}\;=\;\sum_{\nu=1}^{\ell}\alpha_{\nu}p_{\iota^{*}(\nu)}\hskip 10.00002pt\Longleftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\exists\tau\in\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{S}_{\ell_{i}}:\;\iota^{*}=\iota\circ\tau

Since
\ell<n
, this forces
p_{1},\dots,p_{n}\in A(\mathbb{C})
to be pairwise distinct, so the
0
-cycle
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{v}
is reduced and

\mathbb{V}

must be reduced as well. Now let
U\subset\mathbb{P}_{A}
be an open neighborhood of
v
such that the Gauss map
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{|U}\to U
is finite and flat. For the complement of the big diagonal, we then get a bijection

I(\ell,n)\;\longrightarrow\;(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{|U}^{\times\ell}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\ell})_{v},\hskip 10.00002pt\iota\;\longmapsto\;(p_{\iota(1)},\dots,p_{\iota(\ell)},v)

and therefore

\cc(P,\alpha)_{v}\;=\;\frac{1}{N(\alpha)}\cdot\sum_{\iota}[\alpha_{1}p_{\iota(1)}+\cdots+\alpha_{\ell}p_{\iota(\ell)}]\;=\;\frac{1}{N(\alpha)}\cdot(\sigma_{\alpha*}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\times\ell}_{|U}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\ell}))_{v}

which is by definition the fiber of the cycle
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\alpha}
over
v
.
Since this holds for very general
v
it follows that
\cc(P,\alpha)=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\alpha}
as claimed.

If we moreover assume that the cycle
\cc(P,\alpha)
is integral, then it follows from
remark

5.17
that the cycle

\tilde{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}}^{\alpha}\;=\;\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell]}/\mathfrak{S}_{\alpha}

is integral as well. We claim that in this case, the cycle

\mathbb{V}

must be integral. Indeed, suppose for a contradiction that
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{1}+\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{2}
with effective cycles
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{1},\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{2}\in\mathcal{L}(A)
. We already know that the fiber of the Gauss map
\gamma_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{972299}{219436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{694499}{156740}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}}\colon\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\to\mathbb{P}_{A}
over a general point
v\in\mathbb{P}_{A}(\mathbb{C})
is reduced, hence every point in this fiber lies either on
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{1}
or on
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{2}
but not on both. Thus, each point on the big diagonal comes from a point on the big diagonal of one of the two summands. For the complement of the big diagonal, we therefore obtain that

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{|U}^{\times\ell}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\ell}\;\supset\;\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{1|U}^{\times\ell_{1}}\smallsetminus\Delta_{1,\ell})\times_{U}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{2|U}^{\times\ell_{2}}\smallsetminus\Delta_{2,\ell})

where we denote the big diagonals in the summands by
\Delta_{i,\ell}\subset\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{i|U}^{\times\ell_{i}}
. Taking Zariski closure gives

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell]}\;\supset\;\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell_{1},\ell_{2}]}

where

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell_{1},\ell_{2}]}\;:=\;\overline{(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{1|U}^{\times\ell_{1}}\smallsetminus\Delta_{1,\ell})\times_{U}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{2|U}^{\times\ell_{2}}\smallsetminus\Delta_{2,\ell})}.

Now from a look at the degree of the respective Gauss maps we see that
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell_{1},\ell_{2}]}\neq 0
if and only if
0\leqslant\ell_{1}\leqslant\deg(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{1})
and
0\leqslant\ell_{2}\leqslant\deg(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{2})
. For
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell
these four inequalities are equivalent to

\max\{0,\ell-\deg(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{2})\}\leqslant\ell_{1}\leqslant\min\{\deg(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{1}),\ell\}.

This set of inequalities has at least two different solutions
\ell_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
, indeed we have
\max\{0,\ell-\deg(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{2})\}<\min\{\deg(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{1}),\ell\}
because all the occurring degrees of Gauss maps are strictly positive and because
\ell<n=\deg(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}})=\deg(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{1})+\deg(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{2})
. In conclusion, this shows that there are at least two irreducible components of the form
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell_{1},\ell_{2}]}
in
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell]}
. One easily sees that no two such components are related to each other by a permutation of the factors in
A^{\ell}\times\mathbb{P}_{A}
, using again that the fibers of the Gauss maps for
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{1}
and for
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{2}
are disjoint. Hence, the quotient
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell]}/\mathfrak{S}_{\alpha}
has more than one irreducible component, which contradicts our assumption.
∎

5.6.
Weyl orbits for type
B

For
\tilde{G}=\Spin_{2n+1}
, let
\varepsilon_{\pm 1},\dots,\varepsilon_{\pm n}\in\tilde{\mathsf{X}}
be the nontrivial weights of the standard representation of the orthogonal group, with the relations
\varepsilon_{-i}=-\varepsilon_{i}
. The fundamental weights are

\varpi_{i}\;=\;\begin{cases}\varepsilon_{1}+\cdots+\varepsilon_{i}&\textnormal{for $i<n$},\\
\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot(\varepsilon_{1}+\cdots+\varepsilon_{n})&\textnormal{for $i=n$}.\end{cases}

The first
n-1
fundamental weights are again highest weights of wedge powers of the standard representation; the last fundamental weight is the highest weight of the spin representation. The dominant integral weights are the weights of the form

\alpha\;=\;\sum_{\nu=1}^{n}\alpha_{\nu}\varepsilon_{\nu}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{with}\hskip 10.00002pt\alpha_{1}\geqslant\cdots\geqslant\alpha_{n}\geqslant 0,

where
2\alpha_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
are either all even or all odd. Put
\ell=\max\{\nu\mid\alpha_{\nu}\neq 0\}
, and let
I(\ell,\pm n)
be the set of maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota\colon\hskip-11.0pt}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{1,\dots,\ell\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\pm 1,\dots,\pm n\}}

with the property that the map
\nu\mapsto|\iota(\nu)|
is still injective. Then
\alpha
has the Weyl group orbit

W.\alpha\;=\;\bigl\{\alpha_{1}\varepsilon_{\iota(1)}+\cdots+\alpha_{\ell}\varepsilon_{\iota(\ell)}\mid\iota\in I(\ell,\pm n)\bigr\}.

Each weight in this orbit occurs for precisely
N(\alpha)
different choices of
\iota\in I(\ell,\pm n)
, where the number
N(\alpha)
is defined as above; note that different sign choices will lead to different weights and hence the extra signs do not change the count.

In order to translate this back to geometry, we need to adapt
definition

5.16
to the symmetric case:

Definition 5.19
.

Let
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\in\mathcal{L}(A)
be a reduced clean cycle with
[-1]_{\ast}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}
, and let
U\subset\mathbb{P}_{A}
be an open dense subset over which all components of the cycle are finite and flat. For an integer
\ell\geqslant 1
let

\displaystyle\Delta_{\ell}

\displaystyle\;:=\;

\displaystyle\{(p_{1},\dots,p_{\ell},v)\mid p_{i}=p_{j}\;\textnormal{for some $(i,j)$ with $i\neq j$}\}\;\subset\;\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\times\ell}_{|U}

\displaystyle\Delta^{-}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\;:=\;

\displaystyle\{(p_{1},\dots,p_{\ell},v)\mid p_{i}=-p_{j}\;\textnormal{for some $(i,j)$ with $i\neq j$}\}\;\subset\;\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\times\ell}_{|U}

be the big diagonal resp. antidiagonal in the fiber product, and consider the Zariski closure

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell]}_{{{\mathbb{S}}}}\;:=\;\overline{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\times\ell}_{\mid U}\smallsetminus(\Delta_{\ell}\cup\Delta_{\ell}^{-})}\;\subset\;A^{\ell}\times\mathbb{P}_{A}.

For
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{\ell})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\ell}
we obtain a clean cycle

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\alpha}_{{\mathbb{S}}}\;:=\;\frac{1}{N(\alpha)}\cdot\sigma_{\alpha\ast}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[\ell]}_{{\mathbb{S}}})\;\in\;\mathcal{L}(A)

as the pushforward under the morphism

\sigma_{\alpha}\colon A^{\ell}\times\mathbb{P}_{A}\longrightarrow A\times\mathbb{P}_{A},\hskip 10.00002pt(p_{1},\dots,p_{\ell},v)\longmapsto(\alpha_{1}p_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{\ell}p_{\ell},v)

With this notation, we obtain from
theorem

5.11
the following description of the clean cycles corresponding to the dominant weights:

Lemma 5.20
.

Let
G=G_{\omega}(P)
be semisimple with universal cover
\tilde{G}\simeq\Spin_{2n+1}
, and let
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}\varepsilon_{1}+\cdots+\mathbb{Z}\varepsilon_{n}\subset\tilde{\mathsf{X}}
be a dominant weight. If the clean cycle
\cc(P,\alpha)
is reduced, then

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}:=\cc(P,\varepsilon_{1})

is reduced, and

\cc(P,\alpha)\;=\;\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\alpha}_{{{\mathbb{S}}}}

where on the right-hand side we identify
\alpha=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{i}\varepsilon_{i}
with
(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n})
.

Proof.

Let
v\in\mathbb{P}_{A}
be a very general cotangent direction, and let
p_{\pm 1},\dots,p_{\pm n}
be the projection in
A(\mathbb{C})
of the
2n
points in the fiber of the Gauss map
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\to\mathbb{P}_{A}
over this direction, counted with multiplicities so that

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{v}\;=\;[p_{1}]+\cdots+[p_{n}]+[p_{-1}]+\cdots+[p_{-n}]

inside
\mathbb{Z}[A(\mathbb{C})]
. Recall from
theorem

5.11
that the points in the fiber are precisely the images of the weights in the Weyl group orbit
W.d\varepsilon_{1}=\{\pm d\varepsilon_{1},\dots,\pm d\varepsilon_{n}\}\subset\mathsf{X}
under the homomorphism
\varphi_{v}\colon\mathsf{X}\to A(\mathbb{C})
. Up to relabelling indices, we may assume that
\varphi_{v}(\pm d\varepsilon_{i})=p_{\pm i}=\pm p_{i}
for all
i
. Then as in the proof of
lemma

5.18
one obtains

\displaystyle\cc(P,\alpha)_{v}

\displaystyle\;=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N(\alpha)}\cdot\sum_{\iota\in I(\ell,\pm n)}[\alpha_{1}p_{\iota(1)}+\cdots+\alpha_{\ell}p_{\iota(\ell)}].

For very general
v
this
0
-cycle contains no multiple points, since we assumed
\cc(P,\alpha)
to be reduced. The same counting argument as in
lemma

5.18
then shows that for any
\iota,\iota^{*}\in I(\ell,\pm n)
we have the equivalence
