[
FH91
, §20]
):

B_{n}
:

If
N=2n+1
is odd, then the spin representation
\mathbb{S}_{N}
is irreducible.

D_{n}
:

If
N=2n
is even, then
\mathbb{S}_{N}\simeq\mathbb{S}_{N}^{+}\oplus\mathbb{S}_{N}^{-}
splits as the direct sum of two irreducible representations called the
half-spin representations
. They both have dimension
\dim\mathbb{S}_{N}^{+}=\dim\mathbb{S}_{N}^{-}=2^{n-1}
but are not isomorphic to each other, they are only related by an outer automorphism of the spin group. The dual of the half-spin representations and the center of the spin group are given by the following table:

dual of
\mathbb{S}_{N}^{+}

center of
\Spin_{2n}(\mathbb{F})

n
even

\mathbb{S}_{N}^{+}

\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}

n
odd

\mathbb{S}_{N}^{-}

\mathbb{Z}/4\mathbb{Z}

For
n=2m+1
odd, the half-spin representations are faithful. For
n=2m
even, the half-spin representation
\mathbb{S}_{\pm}
is self-dual and the natural pairing is symmetric if
m
is even and alternating if
m
is odd. The images of
\Spin_{4m}(\mathbb{F})
via the half-spin representations

\Spin_{4m}^{\pm}(\mathbb{F})\;\subset\;\GL(\mathbb{S}_{4m}^{\pm})

are called the
half-spin groups
. They are isomorphic to each other and fit in the following diagram of isogenies given by dividing out the subgroups of
Z(\Spin_{4m}(\mathbb{F}))\simeq\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
:

\Spin_{4m}(\mathbb{F})
\Spin_{4m}^{-}(\mathbb{F})
\SO_{4m}(\mathbb{F})
\Spin_{4m}^{+}(\mathbb{F})
\SO_{4m}(\mathbb{F})/\pm 1

We are interested in geometric incarnations of the above:

Definition 8.1
.

Let
X\subset A
be a subvariety. Let
G=G_{X,\omega}^{\ast}
be the derived group of the connected component of the corresponding Tannaka group, and consider the faithful representation
V=\omega(\delta_{X})_{\rvert G}\in\textup{Rep}_{\mathbb{F}}(G)
. Let
n\geqslant 1
be an integer. We say that
X\subset A
is

•

of
spin type
B_{n}
if
G\simeq\Spin_{2n+1}(\mathbb{F})
and
V\simeq\mathbb{S}_{2n+1}
.

•

of
spin type
D_{n}
if
G\simeq\Spin_{2n}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{F})
and
V\simeq\mathbb{S}_{2n}^{\varepsilon}
for some
\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}
.

In both cases, the subvariety
X
is irreducible because the representation
V
is so.

Remark 8.2
.

Suppose that
X
is of spin type
D_{n}
for
n=2m
. Since half-spin representations are self-dual in this case, the subvariety
X
is symmetric up to translation. The Poincaré pairing on
X
is symmetric if
d=\dim X
is even and alternating if
d
is odd. By comparison with the natural pairing on
\mathbb{S}_{n}^{\pm}
, the integers
m
and
d
must have the same parity.

The goal of this section is to show that for smooth subvarieties of small dimension this cannot happen. To state our results, let
g=\dim A
:

Theorem 8.3
.

Let
X\subset A
be a
d
-dimensional nondivisible smooth subvariety with ample normal bundle and
d<(g-1)/2
. Then, for any integer
n\geqslant 1
,

(1)

X
is not of spin type
B_{n}
;

(2)

if
X
is of spin type
D_{n}
, then
d\geqslant(g-1)/4
,
n=2m
with
m\in\{3,\dots,d\}
having the same parity as
d
.

The list of Dynkin types in the above theorem starts with
B_{2}
and
D_{3}
, and at least for these smallest cases the result is optimal in the sense that the dimension bound cannot be weakened:

Example 8.4
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective curve of genus
g=3
, embedded in its Jacobian variety
A=\Alb(X)
. By
[
KW15b
, th. 6.1]
,
[
Wei06
]
there are two cases:

B_{2}
:

If
X
is hyperelliptic, then
G_{X,\omega}^{\ast}\simeq\Sp_{4}(\mathbb{F})
and
\omega(\delta_{X})\simeq\mathbb{F}^{4}
is its standard representation. This representation corresponds to the spin representation under the isomorphism
\Spin_{5}(\mathbb{F})\simeq\Sp_{4}(\mathbb{F})
.

D_{3}
:

If
X
is not hyperelliptic, then
G_{X,\omega}^{\ast}\simeq\SL_{4}(\mathbb{F})
and
\omega(\delta_{X})\simeq\mathbb{F}^{4}
is its standard representation or its dual. These two representations correspond to the two half-spin representations under the isomorphism
\Spin_{6}(\mathbb{F})\simeq\SL_{4}(\mathbb{F})
.

So spin representations do occur, but in this example
2\dim X=\dim A-1
.

8.2.
Structure of the proof

The proof of
theorem

8.3
relies on three independent steps. The first is to show that the structure of spin representations is reflected by characteristic cycles: We say that a cycle
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\in\mathcal{L}(A)
is
symmetric
if
[-1]_{*}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}
. In this case, if the cycle is reduced and effective of Gauss degree
\deg\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}=2n
, then for any integer
r\geqslant 1
we will define via the formalism in
lemma

5.14
a clean effective cycle

\Alt^{r}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\;\in\;\mathcal{L}(A)\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{of Gauss degree}\hskip 10.00002pt\deg\Alt^{r}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\;=\;2^{r}\tbinom{n}{r}.

These cycles are closely related to the wedge powers from
section

7
, for instance we have

\Supp\Alt^{r}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\;=\;\bigcup_{i=0}^{\lfloor r/2\rfloor}\Supp\Alt^{r-2i}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}.

For
r=n
the cycle
\Alt^{n}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}
will correspond to the spin representation.
For half-spin representations we consider the monodromy of the Gauss map
\gamma_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{972299}{219436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{694499}{156740}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}}\colon\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\to\mathbb{P}_{A}
: If the Gauss map has even monodromy in the sense of
definition

5.21
, we will obtain a decomposition

\Alt^{n}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\;=\;\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},+}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}+\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},-}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}

where
\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\pm}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\in\mathcal{L}(A)
are clean effective cycles of Gauss degree
2^{n-1}
. We show:

Theorem 8.5
.

Let
X\subset A
be a smooth nondivisible subvariety of spin type
B_{n}
or
D_{n}
for some
n
. Then there exist
a\in A(k)
, a reduced symmetric effective cycle
Z
on
A
of Gauss degree
\deg\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}=2n
and an integer
e\geqslant 1
with the following properties:

(1)

If
X
is of spin type
B_{n}
, then

\Alt^{n}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{[e](X+a)}
.

(2)

If
X
is of spin type
D_{n}
, then the Gauss map
\gamma_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{995843}{219436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{711317}{156740}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}}
has even monodromy and we have

\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{[e](X+a)}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{for suitable}\hskip 10.00002pt\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}.

In both cases, if
\gamma_{X}\colon\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{X}\to\mathbb{P}_{A}
is a finite morphism, then so is
\gamma_{Z}\colon\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\to\mathbb{P}_{A}
.

For the precise definition of the clean cycles appearing above and the proof of the theorem, we refer to
section

8.3
. The second step will be a dimension estimate for the images

\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}Z\;:=\;\pr_{A}(\Supp(\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z})\;\subset\;A,

for
\varepsilon\in\{+,-,\varnothing\}
. We show:

Theorem 8.6
.

Let
Z
be a reduced symmetric effective cycle on
A
whose Gauss map is a finite morphism of even degree
\deg(\gamma_{Z})=2n
.

(1)

If

\dim\Alt^{n}_{\mathbb{S}}Z<(g-1)/2
, then
n\dim Z<g-1
.

(2)

If the Gauss map
\gamma_{Z}
has even monodromy and there is
\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}
for which the dimension
d:=\dim\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}Z
satisfies

d<\begin{cases}(g-1)/4&\text{if $n=2m$ is even and $m\leqslant d+1$},\\
(g-1)/2&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

then
n\dim Z<g-1
.

The proof of this is given in
section

8.4
. Finally, the last step for ruling out spin representations will be to show that in the given dimension range, the cycle
\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}
cannot be smooth and integral. More precisely:

Theorem 8.7
.

Let
X\subset A
be a smooth nondivisible subvariety with ample normal bundle and
Z
a reduced symmetric effective cycle on
A
whose Gauss map is finite of even degree
\deg(\gamma_{Z})=2n\geqslant 4
. Suppose that for some integer
e\geqslant 1
one of the following two conditions holds:

(1)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{[e](X)}=\Alt^{n}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}
.

(2)

\gamma_{Z}
has even monodromy and
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{[e](X)}=\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}
for some
\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}
.

Then
n\dim Z\geqslant\dim A
.

Proof of
theorem

8.3
.

Let
X\subset A
be a
d
-dimensional smooth nondivisible subvariety of spin type
B_{n}
or
D_{n}
for some integer
n
with
d<(g-1)/2
. After replacing
X
by a translate, there exists by
theorem

8.5
an integer
e\geqslant 1
and a reduced symmetric effective cycle
Z
on
A
with
\deg(\gamma_{Z})=2n
such that

\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\;=\;\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{[e](X)}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{for some}\hskip 10.00002pt\varepsilon\;\in\;\{+,-,\varnothing\}.

It follows that

\dim\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}Z\;=\;\dim[e](X)\;=\;\dim X.

Note that we have
\varepsilon\neq\varnothing
only in spin type
D_{n}
and in that case
\gamma_{Z}
has even monodromy.
Moreover, if
X
is of spin type
D_{n}
,
n=2m
is even and
m\leqslant d+1
, assume
d<(g-1)/4
. Then
theorem

8.6
implies

n\dim Z<\dim A
, which contradicts
theorem

8.7
. The remaining cases are of type
D_{n}
with
n=2m
even,
d\geqslant(g-1)/4
and
m\leqslant d+1
.
Remark

8.2
implies that
d-m
must be even.
Lemmas

2.12
and
2.13
imply that in the current dimension range the absolute value of the topological Euler characteristic of
X
is never
8
, so the case
m=2
does not occur.
∎

8.3.
Characteristic cycles of spin representations

We now explain how to compute characteristic cycles for subvarieties of spin type. Consider a symmetric reduced clean effective cycle
Z
on
A
with dominant Gauss map
\gamma_{Z}\colon\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\to\mathbb{P}_{A}
. For an integer
r\geqslant 1
we put

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}}}^{[r]}\;:=\;\overline{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z|U}^{\times r}\smallsetminus(\Delta_{r}\cup\Delta_{r}^{-})}\;\subset\;\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}^{\times r}

as in
definition

5.16
, where
U\subset\mathbb{P}_{A}
is any open dense subset over which the Gauss map
\gamma_{Z}
is finite and étale. For a partition
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{r})
consider the sum morphism

\sigma_{\alpha}\colon A^{r}\times\mathbb{P}_{A}\longrightarrow A\times\mathbb{P}_{A},\hskip 20.00003pt(z_{1},\dots,z_{r},\xi)\longmapsto(\alpha_{1}z_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{r}z_{r},\xi).

We put

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}}}^{\alpha}\;:=\;\sigma_{\alpha*}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}}}^{[r]}).

We are mostly interested in the special case of the partition
\alpha=(1^{r})=(1,\dots,1)
and write

\Alt^{r}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\;:=\;\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}}}^{(1^{r})}

in this case. As in
definition

5.21
, we say that the Gauss map
\gamma_{Z}\colon\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\to\mathbb{P}_{A}
has
even monodromy
if its degree is an even integer
\deg(\gamma_{Z})=2n
and the finite étale cover
\gamma_{Z|U}\colon\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z|U}\to U
has as its monodromy group a subgroup of
(\pm 1)^{n}_{+}\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{n}
. In this case

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}}}^{[n]}\;=\;\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}},+}^{[n]}+\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}},-}^{[n]}

for
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}},\pm}^{[n]}
as in
definition

5.21
, and for partitions
\alpha
of length
r=n
we put

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}},\pm}^{\alpha}\;=\;\sigma_{\alpha*}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}},\pm}^{[n]}).

Again for
\alpha=(1^{n})
we instead write
\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\pm}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}:=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}},\pm}^{(1^{n})}
.

Proof of
theorem

8.5
.

Replacing
X\subset A
by a translate we may assume
\det(\delta_{X})=\delta_{0}
. As in the proof of
theorem

7.4
, but replacing
lemma

5.18
by
lemma

5.20
, we then find a reduced symmetric effective cycle
Z
on
A
with
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\in\langle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{X}\rangle
such that

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{[e](X)}\;=\;\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\deg(\gamma_{Z})\;=\;2n

for some integer
e\geqslant 1
and
\varepsilon\in\{+,-,\varnothing\}
as claimed.
∎

8.4.
Dimension estimates in the spin case

Let
Z
be a symmetric reduced effective cycle in
A
.
As before, we put

\displaystyle\Alt^{r}_{{\mathbb{S}}}Z\;

\displaystyle:=\;\pr_{A}(\Supp\Alt^{r}_{{\mathbb{S}}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z})\;\subset\;A,

\displaystyle\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}Z\;

\displaystyle:=\;\pr_{A}(\Supp\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z})\;\subset\;A.

We want to show that under certain assumptions the dimension of these images grows linearly with
r
in a suitable range. The first step is to relate the cycles associated with the two half-spin representations:

Lemma 8.8
.

Let
Z\subset A
be a symmetric reduced effective cycle whose Gauss map
\gamma_{Z}
is finite of degree
\deg(\gamma_{Z})=2n\geqslant 4
and has even monodromy. Suppose that
n
is odd, or that
n=2m
is even and
m-1>\min_{\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}}\dim\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}Z
. Then,

\dim\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},+}Z=\dim\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},-}Z.

Proof.

We may suppose
k=\mathbb{C}
. For
\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}
set

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{\varepsilon}:=\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z},\hskip 20.00003ptX_{\varepsilon}=\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}Z=\pr_{A}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{\varepsilon}).

If
n=2m+1
is odd, then the definitions imply
X_{+}=[-1]_{*}X_{-}
and the statement follows. The representation-theoretic analogue of the previous identity is that the half-spin representations
\mathbb{S}_{+}
and
\mathbb{S}_{-}
are dual to each other. Suppose henceforth that
n=2m
is even and, up to changing signs, that we have
\dim X_{+}\leqslant\dim X_{-}
. With this notation
m-1>d:=\dim X_{+}
. Similarly, drawing inspiration from the isomorphism of representations
\Alt^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{+})\simeq\Alt^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{-})
we obtain the following:

Claim 8.9
.

The following identity of Lagrangian cycles holds:

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{+}\circ\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{+}-[2]_{\ast}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{+}=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{-}\circ\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{-}-[2]_{\ast}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{2032337}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1662625}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1091722}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{-}\in\mathcal{L}(A).

Proof of the claim.

Pick a general cotangent direction
v\in\mathbb{P}_{A}(k)
, and denote by

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,v}\;=\;\{\pm p_{1},\ldots,\pm p_{n}\}\;\subset\;A

the corresponding fiber of the Gauss map, which we identify as a
0
-cycle on the abelian variety via the projection
\pr_{A}\colon\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\to A
. Let
\{\pm 1\}^{n}_{\varepsilon}\subset\{\pm 1\}^{n}
be the subset made of
n
-tuples
a=(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})
such that the sign of
a_{1}\cdots a_{n}
is
\varepsilon
. As
0
-cycles on
A
, we have

\displaystyle(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{\varepsilon})_{v}\;

\displaystyle=\;\sum_{a\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}_{\varepsilon}}[a_{1}p_{1}+\cdots+a_{n}p_{n}]\in\textup{Z}_{0}(A),

\displaystyle(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{\varepsilon}\circ\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{\varepsilon})_{v}\;

\displaystyle=\;\sum_{a,b\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}_{\varepsilon}}[(a_{1}+b_{1})p_{1}+\cdots+(a_{n}+b_{n})p_{n}]\in\textup{Z}_{0}(A),

where the summation sign refers to the sum as cycles and
[x]
is the
0
-cycle given by a point
x\in A(k)
. We split the sum in the preceding equation in two: The sum ranging on couples
(a,b)
with
a=b
gives the
0
-cycle
([2]_{\ast}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{\varepsilon})_{v}
. For the remaining couples, notice that we have a bijection

\left\{\left.(a,b)\in(\{\pm 1\}^{n}_{+})^{2}\,\right|\,a\neq b\right\}\longrightarrow\left\{\left.(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in(\{\pm 1\}^{n}_{-})^{2}\,\right|\,a^{\prime}\neq b^{\prime}\right\}

sending
(a,b)
to the couple
(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
obtained by changing the sign of the first entry in which
a
and
b
differ. Such a bijection is compatible with sum, that is
a+b=a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}
. Letting vary
v
gives the desired identity of cycles.
∎

The identity in the claim can be rewritten as:

(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{+}+\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{-})\circ(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{+}-\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{-})=[2]_{\ast}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{+}-\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{-}).

Since
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}
has finite Gauss map, so do
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{+}
and
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{-}
by construction. By
lemma

5.5
we obtain, by passing to Segre classes,

s(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{+}+\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{-})\ast s(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{+}-\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{-})=[2]_{\ast}s(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{+}-\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{-})\in\CH_{<g}(A).

Let
s
and
\delta
be the image of
s(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{+})
and
s(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{+}-\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{-})
in the homology
\textup{H}_{\bullet}(A,\mathbb{Z})
, so that

(2s+\delta)\ast\delta=[2]_{\ast}\delta\in\textup{H}_{\bullet}(A,\mathbb{Z}),

where
\ast
denotes the Pontryagin product in homology. Write
s_{i},\delta_{i}\in\textup{H}_{2i}(A,\mathbb{Z})
for the pieces of
s,\delta
in degree
2i
. Then in degree
2r
the previous identity reads as

\sum_{i=0}^{r}(2s_{i}+\delta_{i})\ast\delta_{r-i}=2^{2r}\delta_{r}\in\textup{H}_{2r}(A,\mathbb{Z}),

because the multiplication by
2
on
A
acts as multiplying by
2^{2r}
on
\textup{H}_{2r}(A,\mathbb{Z})
. Now for
\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}
, the
0
-th Segre class of
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{\varepsilon}
has degree

\deg\gamma_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{972299}{219436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{694499}{156740}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{\varepsilon}}=|\{\pm 1\}^{n}_{\varepsilon}|=2^{2m-1}.

Plugging in the identities
s_{0}=2^{2m-1}
and
\delta_{0}=0
obtained in this way yields the recursion formula

(2^{2r}-2^{2m})\delta_{r}=\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}(2s_{i}+\delta_{i})\ast\delta_{r-i}\in\textup{H}_{2r}(A,\mathbb{Z}).

Then the vanishing of
\delta_{0}
inductively implies

\delta_{r}=0,\hskip 20.00003ptr=0,\dots,m-1.

Suppose by contradiction
\dim X_{-}>d=\dim X_{+}
. Then

\delta_{d+1}=s_{d+1}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{-})-s_{d+1}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{+})=s_{d+1}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{-})\neq 0

where we abusively identified Segre classes with their images in homology. Then the vanishing of
\delta_{r}
for
r<m
implies
d+1\geqslant m
, contradicting
m-1>d
.
∎

The second step is the following analog of
lemma

7.11
(note that at first we only get a weaker estimate in the spin case since we here only start from a dimension bound on the support of
\Alt^{r}_{\mathbb{S}}Z
, which a priori might be strictly smaller than the one of
\Alt^{r}Z
):

Proposition 8.10
.

Let
Z
be a symmetric reduced effective cycle on
A
whose Gauss map has degree
\deg(\gamma_{Z})=2n\geqslant 4
.

(1)

If
\dim\Alt^{n}_{\mathbb{S}}Z<(g-1)/2
, then
\dim\Alt^{i}_{\mathbb{S}}Z<g-1
for
i=1,\dots,n-1
.

(2)

If
\gamma_{Z}
is finite and has even monodromy and if there is
\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}
such that the dimension
d:=\dim\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}Z
satifies

d<\begin{cases}(g-1)/4&\text{if $n=2m$ is even and $m\leqslant d+1$},\\
(g-1)/2&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

then
\dim\Alt^{i}_{{\mathbb{S}}}Z<g-1
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
and
\dim\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}}}Z<\frac{g-1}{2}
if
n
is odd.

Proof.

We may suppose
k=\mathbb{C}
. (1) Put

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s)}:=\Alt^{s}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}
. Pick a general cotangent direction
v\in\mathbb{P}_{A}(k)
, and denote by

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,v}\;=\;\{\pm p_{1},\ldots,\pm p_{n}\}\;\subset\;A

the corresponding fiber of the Gauss map, which we identify as usual with a set of points on the abelian variety via the projection
\pr_{A}\colon\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\to A
. Writing
p_{-i}:=-p_{i}
we have

\displaystyle\Supp(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s)})_{v}\;

\displaystyle=\;\{p_{i_{1}}+\cdots+p_{i_{s}}\mid|i_{1}|,\dots,|i_{s}|\textrm{ pairwise distinct }\},

\displaystyle\Supp(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(n)}\circ\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(n)})_{v}\;

\displaystyle=\;\left\{\left.\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\delta_{i}+\varepsilon_{i})p_{i}\right|\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n},\varepsilon_{1},\dots,\varepsilon_{n}\in\{\pm 1\}\right\}.

By specializing to the case
\delta_{i}=\pm\varepsilon_{i}
and varying the cotangent direction
v\in\mathbb{P}_{A}(k)
we find

\Supp\left([2]_{\ast}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(1)}+[2]_{\ast}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(2)}+\cdots+[2]_{\ast}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(n)}\right)\;\subset\;\Supp(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(n)}\circ\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(n)}).

Hence, for the images
Z^{(s)}:=\pr_{A}(\Supp(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s)}))=\Supp(\Alt^{s}_{\mathbb{S}}Z)\subset A
we obtain the inclusions

[2]\left(Z\cup Z^{(2)}\cup\cdots\cup Z^{(n)}\right)\;\subset\;\pr_{A}(\Supp(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(n)}\circ\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(r)}))\subset Z^{(n)}+Z^{(n)}.

Since
[2]\colon A\to A
is an isogeny, a look at dimension then shows that for
1\leqslant s\leqslant n
we have

\dim Z^{(s)}\leqslant\dim(Z^{(n)}+Z^{(n)})\leqslant 2\dim(Z^{(n)})<g-1

where the last inequality holds by our dimension assumption. This proves (1).

(2) If
n=2m+1
is odd or
n=2m
is even with
m>d+1
, then
lemma

8.8
implies

d=\dim\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},+}=\dim\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},-}=\dim\Alt^{n}_{\mathbb{S}}Z

so we are done by (1). Now assume
n=2m
and
m-1\leqslant d<(g-1)/4
. In that case, a set-theoretic look at the fibers of Gauss maps gives

\Supp\left([2]_{\ast}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(2)}+[2]_{\ast}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(4)}+\cdots+[2]_{\ast}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(n-2)}\right)\;\subset\;\Supp(\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\circ\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}).

For all
i<m
then

\dim Z^{(2i)}\;\leqslant\;2\dim\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\;<\;(g-1)/2,

where the second inequality holds by assumption. Since
\Alt^{2}Z=\Alt^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}Z+\Alt^{0}_{\mathbb{S}}Z
it follows that

\dim\Alt^{2}Z<(g-1)/2
,
and the monotonicity of usual wedge powers in
lemma

7.11
