gives

\dim Z\;=\;\dim\Alt^{1}Z\;\leqslant\;\dim\Alt^{2}Z\;<\;(g-1)/2,

since we assumed the Gauss map to be finite. Then
\Supp\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(2i+1)}\subset\Supp\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(2i)}\circ\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(1)}
also implies
\dim Z^{(2i+1)}\leqslant\dim Z^{(2i)}+\dim Z^{(1)}<(g-1)/2+(g-1)/2=g-1
for all
i<m
. Likewise,
\Supp\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(n)}\subset\Supp\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(n-2)}\circ\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(2)}
gives
\dim Z^{(n)}<g-1
.
∎

Proof of
theorem

8.6
.

Let
Z
be a reduced symmetric effective cycle on
A
whose Gauss map is a finite morphism of degree
\deg(\gamma_{Z})=2n
for some integer
n\geqslant 2
. Assume that we are in one of the following two cases:

(1)

\dim\Alt^{n}_{\mathbb{S}}Z<(g-1)/2
, or

(2)

n=2m
is even, the Gauss map
\gamma_{Z}
has even monodromy, and for some
\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}
we have

\dim\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}<(g-1)/4.

By
proposition

8.10
then

\dim\Alt^{i}_{\mathbb{S}}Z<g-1

for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
,
so it will be enough to show that

\dim\Alt^{i}_{\mathbb{S}}Z\;=\;i\dim Z

for all those
i
. For
i=1
there is nothing to show. We now use induction: Suppose that

\dim\Alt^{i}_{\mathbb{S}}Z\;=\;i\dim Z\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{for all}\hskip 10.00002pti\;\in\;\{1,2,\dots,s-1\},

where
s\leqslant r
is a positive integer. We want to conclude
\dim\Alt^{s}_{\mathbb{S}}Z=s\dim Z
.

We may assume
\dim Z>0
. For simplicity, we put
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\beta}:=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}}}^{\alpha}
where
\beta=\alpha^{t}
denotes the transpose of a partition
\alpha
, extending our notation from the previous proof. The definitions imply

(8.1)

\Supp\left(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s-1)}\circ\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(1)}\right)=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s)}\cup\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s-1,1)}\cup\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s-2)}.

We now compare dimensions. For the last two pieces on the right-hand side, the inclusions
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s-1,1)}\subset\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{2Z}\circ\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s-2)}
and
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s-2)}\subset\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\circ\cdots\circ\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}
(with
s-2
factors) imply

\displaystyle\dim\pr_{A}\left(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s-1,1)}\cup\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s-2)}\right)

\displaystyle\;\leqslant\;(s-1)\dim Z

\displaystyle\;=\;\dim\Alt^{s-1}_{\mathbb{S}}Z

by our induction assumption

\displaystyle\;<\;g-1

by
proposition

8.10
as
s-1\leqslant n
.

Since
\dim Z>0
, it follows that

(8.2)

\displaystyle\dim\pr_{A}\left(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s-1,1)}\cup\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s-2)}\right)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\min\{s\dim Z,g-1\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dim\pr_{A}\left(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s-1)}\circ\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(1)}\right),

where the last equality holds by
corollary

5.7
since by definition
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(1)}=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}
and by induction
\dim\pr_{A}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s-1)})=\dim\Alt^{s-1}_{\mathbb{S}}Z=(s-1)\dim Z
. Note that the corollary does not require integrality of the occurring clean cycles; we only need that each irreducible component of their support has finite Gauss map, which follows from our assumption that the Gauss map
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\to\mathbb{P}_{A}
is a finite morphism.
Comparing (
8.1
) and (
8.2
) we conclude

\dim\pr_{A}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s)})=\min\{s\dim Z,g-1\}.

But
\dim\pr_{A}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s)})<g-1
again by
proposition

8.10
because
s\leq n
. Hence, it follows that

\dim\pr_{A}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{(s)})\;=\;s\dim Z

which completes the induction step.
∎

8.5.
Spin representations do not occur in small dimension

It remains to show that smooth nondivisible subvarieties of small enough dimension are not of spin type. Recall the setting of
theorem

8.7
:
We are given an integral subvariety
X\subset A
and a reduced symmetric effective cycle
Z
on
A
such that

•

the subvariety
X\subset A
is smooth and nondivisible,

•

the Gauss maps
\gamma_{X}
,
\gamma_{Z}
are finite, and
\deg(\gamma_{Z})=2n
for an integer
n\geqslant 2
,

•

we have

\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{[e](X)}

for some
e\geqslant 1
and suitable
\varepsilon\in\{+,-,\varnothing\}
.

Here we make the convention that the labels
\varepsilon=\pm
will only be used if
\gamma_{Z}
has even monodromy, so that the corresponding cycles are defined.
We want to show that in the above situation
n\dim Z\geqslant\dim A
:

Proof of
theorem

8.7
.

Suppose by contradiction that
n\dim Z<\dim A
. Let
Z^{\prime}\subset A
be an irreducible component of maximal dimension in
Z
. The finiteness of the Gauss map implies by
theorem

2.8
that
Z^{\prime}\subset A
is nondegenerate. Hence,
corollary

7.10
gives

\frac{\deg(\gamma_{Z^{\prime}})+2}{2}\,\dim Z\;\geqslant\;\dim A.

This is not quite strong enough to contradict our assumption, but it allows us to reduce to the case of symmetric components: If
Z^{\prime}\neq-Z^{\prime}
were not symmetric, then the effective symmetric cycle
Z
would contain the two distinct components
\pm Z^{\prime}
and these two components clearly have the same Gauss degree, which would lead to the estimate

2n=\deg(\gamma_{Z})\geqslant\deg(\gamma_{Z^{\prime}})+\deg(\gamma_{-Z^{\prime}})=2\deg(\gamma_{Z^{\prime}})
.
The previous inequality then leads to

\frac{n+2}{2}\dim Z\;\geqslant\;\dim A

and for
n\geqslant 2
this contradicts our assumption that
n\dim Z<\dim A
.

So for the remainder of the proof we will assume that the subvariety
Z^{\prime}=-Z^{\prime}
is symmetric.
Since by assumption
n\dim Z^{\prime}<\dim A
, it follows from
proposition

7.9
that the morphism

\pr_{Z^{\prime},n}\colon\Supp\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z^{\prime}}^{[n]}\longrightarrow{Z^{\prime}}^{n}

is surjective. Let
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\prime}\subset\Supp\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z^{\prime}}^{[n]}
be an irreducible component dominating
Z^{\prime n}
. This component is not contained in the union
\Delta\cup\Delta^{-}\subset A^{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{A}
of the big diagonal and the big antidiagonal, hence

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\prime}\;\subset\;\Supp\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}}}^{[n]}.

If the Gauss map has even monodromy, then moreover
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}}}^{[n]}=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}},+}^{[n]}+\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}},-}^{[n]}
and, by irreducibility,

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\prime}\;\subset\;\Supp\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}},\delta}^{[n]}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{for some}\hskip 10.00002pt\delta\;\in\;\{+,-\}.

To adjust the signs, use the involution

\varphi:=\id^{n-1}\times[-1]\colon A^{n}\to A^{n}

and consider the subvariety

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\;\subset\;\Supp\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{[n]}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{defined by}\hskip 10.00002pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}\;:=\;\begin{cases}\;\varphi_{*}\,\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\prime}&\textnormal{if $\varepsilon=-\delta$},\\[1.99997pt]
\;\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}^{\prime}&\textnormal{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Since
Z^{\prime}=-Z^{\prime}
, we still have

\pr_{A^{n}}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}})\;=\;(Z^{\prime})^{n-1}\times(\pm Z^{\prime})\;=\;(Z^{\prime})^{n}.

By construction,

\mathbb{V}

is an irreducible component of
\Supp\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}^{[n]}
and by assumption we have

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Y}\;=\;\Alt^{n}_{{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z}\;:=\;\frac{1}{N(\alpha)}\,\sigma_{\alpha*}\bigl(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,{\mathbb{S}},\varepsilon}^{[n]}\bigr)\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{for}\hskip 10.00002pt\alpha\;=\;(1^{n}),

so the irreducibility of the left-hand side forces
\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Y}=\Supp\sigma_{\alpha*}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}})
. This gives a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Z^{\prime})^{n}}

\mathbb{V}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{${\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}_{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{Z,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{Z,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{Y}}

where
\sigma
is the sum morphism, which is generically finite by
lemma

2.7
.

In fact,
\sigma
must be finite by the same argument as in
proposition

7.12
: The fibers of
\pr_{Y}
are of pure dimension
N=\codim_{A}Y-1
by
corollary

2.5
. Moreover,
\sigma_{\alpha}
is finite since the rightmost square in the above diagram commutes and since in that square
\gamma_{Z,r}
and
\gamma_{Y}
are finite morphisms by our finiteness assumptions on Gauss maps. So all fibers of
\pr_{Y}\circ\sigma_{\alpha}\colon\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1984290}{447828}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1580154}{313481}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{1345330}{222744}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathbb{V}$}}$}}}}}_{Z,r}\to Y
are of dimension
N
. Since
\sigma
is generically finite, it follows from the commutativity of the leftmost square in the above diagram that the generic fiber of the morphism
\pr_{Z,r}
has dimension
N
as well. We can now argue by contradiction: Any positive-dimensional fiber of
\sigma
would give rise to a fiber of
\pr_{Y}\circ\sigma_{\alpha}
of dimension
\geqslant N+1
, by semi-continuity of dimension of fibers for proper morphisms
[
Sta22
,
lemma 0D4I
]
. This shows that
\sigma
is finite.

But
Z^{\prime}=-Z^{\prime}
is symmetric and
\dim Z^{\prime}>0
, hence looking at antidiagonals one sees that the sum morphism
\sigma
cannot be finite. Contradiction.
∎

Appendix A
Reduction to the complex case

For reference we include the following well-known fact about
\ell
-adic constructible sheaves with coefficients in
\mathbb{F}=\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
on varieties over an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic zero:

Lemma A.1
.

Let
k\subset K
be an extension of algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero and let
X
be a variety over
k
. Then the base change functor

(-)_{K}\colon{\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}}(X,\mathbb{F})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}}(X_{K},\mathbb{F})

is fully faithful. Moreover, for every
P\in{\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}}(X_{K},\mathbb{F})
there exists a subfield
k^{\prime}\subset K
which is the algebraic closure of a finitely generated extension of
k
such that
P
is in the
essential image of the functor

(-)_{K}\colon{\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}}(X_{k^{\prime}},\mathbb{F})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}}(X_{K},\mathbb{F}).

Proof.

For the full faithfulness, consider two complexes
P,P^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}}(X,\mathbb{F})
. To see
\Hom_{{\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}}(X,\mathbb{F})}(P,P^{\prime})\simeq\Hom_{{\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}}(X_{K},\mathbb{F})}(P_{K},P^{\prime}_{K})
, we only need to take
Q=R\mathcal{H}om(P,P^{\prime})
in the isomorphism

H^{\bullet}(X,Q)\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\;H^{\bullet}(X_{K},Q_{K})

which is obtained by base change (see e.g.
[
Mil80
, cor. VI.4.3]
for the case of étale torsion sheaves, the case of
\ell
-adic sheaf complexes then follows formally).

Now let
P\in{\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}}(X_{K},\mathbb{F})
. We want to show that it descends to a subfield
k^{\prime}\subset K
which is the algebraic closure of a finitely generated extension of
k
. We use induction on the number of nonvanishing cohomology sheaves. Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}
be maximal with
\mathcal{H}^{m}(P)\neq 0
, and consider the triangle

\tau_{<m}(P)\longrightarrow P\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{m}(P)[-m]\longrightarrow

Rotating the triangle, we obtain

P\;\simeq\;\cone(\mathcal{H}^{m}(P)[1-m]\to\tau_{<m}(P))

If the source and the target of a morphism descend to a given subfield, then so does the morphism by full faithfulness, and hence also the cone descends to the same subfield. By induction, it therefore suffices to discuss the case where
P
is a single constructible
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-sheaf. Then by
[
SGA

⁤
4

1
2

, Rapport, prop. 2.5]
, there is an open dense subset of
X_{K}
on which
P
is smooth. Let
k^{\prime}\subset K
be the algebraic closure of a finitely generated extension of
k
such that the open dense subset has the form
j_{K}\colon U_{K}\to X_{K}
for some open
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X_{k^{\prime}}
. Looking at the adjunction morphism

j_{K!}j_{K}^{*}(P)\longrightarrow P

and arguing by induction on
\dim\Supp(P)
it will suffice to show that
j_{K}^{*}(P)\simeq L_{K}
for some local system
L
on
U
. But this is clear because of the equivalence between
\ell
-adic local systems and representations of the étale fundamental group
[
SGA

⁤
4

1
2

, Rapport, prop. 2.4]
and the invariance of the geometric étale fundamental group
[
SGA 1
, Exp. XIII, prop. 4.6]
.
∎

Appendix B
Symmetric powers of varieties

Let
X
be a variety over an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic
0
, and fix an integer
n\geqslant 1
. The
n
-fold symmetric product
of
X
is defined as the categorical quotient

\Sym^{n}X\;:=\;X^{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}

of
X^{n}=X\times\cdots\times X
by the permutation action of the symmetric group
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
. This quotient exists for instance if
X
is quasiprojective
[
BLR90
, §9.3, p. 253]
.
In that case, we denote by

\pi_{X}\colon\hskip 10.00002ptX^{n}\;\longrightarrow\;\Sym^{n}X

the quotient morphism. Let
U\subset X^{n}
be the complement of the big diagonal, i.e., the open subset of all
n
-tuples of pairwise distinct points. Then
\pi_{X}(U)
is open in
\Sym^{n}X
and
\pi_{X}\colon U\to\pi_{X}(U)
is a principal
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
-bundle
[
SGA 3
, exp. V, th. 4.1]
, in particular

\deg\pi_{X}=n!.

On the other hand,
\pi_{X}
is not étale at any point
x\in(X^{n}\smallsetminus U)(k)
.

Proposition B.1
.

Let
X
be a quasiprojective variety. If
X
is reduced, irreducible, integral or normal, then the respective property holds also for
\Sym^{n}X
.

Proof.

If
X
has one of the stated properties, then
X^{n}
has the same property since
k
is algebraically closed. Therefore, the claim follows from the fact that the properties are stable under categorical quotients
[
MFK94
, p. 5]
.
∎

Proposition B.2
.

If
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
is a finite birational morphism between integral quasiprojective varieties, then
\Sym^{n}f\colon\Sym^{n}X^{\prime}\to\Sym^{n}X
is finite birational.

Proof.

In the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sym^{n}X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sym^{n}X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sym^{n}f}

the morphisms
f^{n}
,
\pi_{X^{\prime}}
and
\pi_{X}
are finite, hence
\Sym^{n}f
is also finite. Since
f^{n}
is birational, we obtain

\deg(\Sym^{n}f\circ\pi_{X^{\prime}})=\deg(\pi_{X}\circ f^{n})=n!=\deg(\pi_{X})

which implies that the morphism
\Sym^{n}f
has degree
1
as required.
∎

Proposition B.3
.

Let
X
be an integral quasiprojective variety with
\dim X>0
, and let
n\geqslant 2
be an integer. Then

\text{$\Sym^{n}X$ is smooth}\iff\text{$X$ is a smooth curve}.

Proof.

It is well known that for any smooth curve
X
the symmetric powers
\Sym^{n}X
are smooth
[
BLR90
, p. 255]
. Conversely, assume that
\Sym^{n}X
is smooth. First, we show that
\dim X=1
. For this, we may replace
X
by its smooth locus and thus assume
X
is smooth. By Nagata-Zariski purity
[
SGA 1
, th. X.3.1]
, the branch locus
B
of
\pi_{X}\colon X^{n}\to\Sym^{n}X
is empty or a divisor in
\Sym^{n}X
. On the other hand, the morphism
\pi_{X}
is ramified at a
k
-point
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})
of
X^{n}
if and only if
x_{i}=x_{j}
for some
i\neq j
, that is, if and only if
x
lies in the big diagonal
\Delta_{n}
of
X^{n}
. Since
n\geqslant 2
, the big diagonal is nonempty, so that the branch locus is a divisor and thus

n\dim X-1=\dim B=\dim\Delta_{n}=(n-1)\dim X,

hence
\dim X=1
. Having shown that
X
is a curve, we now verify that it must be smooth. Suppose to the contrary that there exists a singular point
x_{1}\in X(k)
. Pick pairwise distinct points
x_{2},\dots,x_{n}\in X(k)\smallsetminus\{x_{1}\}
. Then the morphism
\pi_{X}
is étale at
x=(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})
, so
\pi_{X}(x)
is a singular point in
\Sym^{n}X
, a contradiction.
∎

In
section

1.3
we needed a criterion for a birational morphism from a symmetric power of a smooth variety to another smooth variety to be an isomorphism. The proof is naturally cast for certain singularities:
To define them, recall that for a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on a variety
V
and an integer
m\geqslant 1
we write
\mathcal{F}^{[m]}
for the reflexive hull of
\mathcal{F}^{\otimes m}
. A reflexive sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
V
of generic rank one is a
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
if there is
m\geqslant 1
such that
\mathcal{F}^{[m]}
is a line bundle. When
V
is proper, such a sheaf
\mathcal{F}
is
nef
if the line bundle
\mathcal{F}^{[m]}
is. For
V
normal, the
canonical sheaf

\mathcal{K}_{V}
is defined as the pushforward to
V
of the canonical bundle on
V^{\textup{reg}}
and is reflexive of generic rank one. By definition
V
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Gorenstein
if it is normal and the canonical sheaf is a
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle. This is the case if
V
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial
, i.e. if
V
is normal and any reflexive sheaf of generic rank one on
V
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle. The singularities of
V
are
terminal
if
V
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Gorenstein and the pullback of any local section of
\mathcal{K}_{V}^{[m]}
for any
m\geqslant 1
vanishes along all the components of the exceptional divisor of any resolution of
V
; see
[
KM98
, def. 2.34]
. With this terminology we have:

Proposition B.4
.

Let
X
and
W
be
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial normal, integral projective varieties. Suppose that
W
has terminal singularities.
If
\mathcal{K}_{X}
is nef and
\dim X\geqslant 2
, then any proper birational morphism
f\colon\Sym^{n}X\to W
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The symmetric product
S:=\Sym^{n}X
of
X
is normal by
proposition

B.1
and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial by
[
KM98
, lemma 5.16]
. The hypothesis
\dim X\geqslant 2
implies that the quotient morphism
\pi\colon X^{n}\to S
is unramified in codimension one, hence the natural morphism
(\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{S})^{\vee\vee}\to\mathcal{K}_{X^{n}}
is an isomorphism. Thus
\mathcal{K}_{S}
is nef since
\mathcal{K}_{X^{n}}
is so. To conclude apply
lemma

B.5
below with
V=S
.
∎

To keep track of the arguments that enter the proof, we state the lemma in a generality which is slightly broader than actually needed. To do this, for a proper morphism
f\colon V\to W
a
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
V
is said to be
f
-nef
if the restriction of
\mathcal{L}^{[m]}
to any fiber of
f
is nef, where
m\geqslant 1
is such that
\mathcal{L}^{[m]}
is a line bundle.

Lemma B.5
.

Let
f\colon V\to W
be a proper birational morphism between normal quasiprojective varieties. Suppose that
V
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Gorenstein,
\mathcal{K}_{V}
is
f
-nef and
W
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial with terminal singularities. Then
f
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By assumption the varieties
V
and
W
are
\mathbb{Q}
-Gorenstein, thus there is an integer
m\geqslant 1
such that
\mathcal{K}_{V}^{[m]}
and
\mathcal{K}_{W}^{[m]}
are line bundles. Write

\mathcal{K}_{V}^{[m]}=f^{*}\mathcal{K}_{W}^{[m]}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{V}(E)

for some Cartier divisor
E
on
V
. Since
W
has terminal singularities, the divisor
E
is effective and its support is exactly the divisorial part of the exceptional locus of
f
. Moreover
E
is
f
-nef: Indeed, for any projective curve
C\subset V
contracted by
f
we have

E.C=\mathcal{K}_{V}^{[m]}.C-f^{*}\mathcal{K}_{W}^{[m]}.C=\mathcal{K}_{V}^{[m]}.C\geqslant 0

because
\mathcal{K}_{V}
is
f
-nef. Therefore
-E
is effective by
[
KM98
, lemma 3.39 (1)]
, hence trivial because
E
is effective. It follows that the exceptional set of
f
has no divisorial part. On the other hand
W
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, so the exceptional locus of
f
is pure of codimension one
[
Deb01
, 1.40]
. Thus,
f
is an isomorphism.
∎
